I don’t want to make too much of it, but for months now I’ll get kind of fired up, like everything’s going to work out and I can take on whatever’s in front of me.\n\nAnd then other times I’m more… touchy, I guess. Snapping over little things at home or out in the yard, stuff that normally wouldn’t get to me. That’s not really how I like to be, so I’ve been trying to keep a lid on it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you get into those fired-up periods, do you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I do. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m out partying or anything, but most nights I’ll sleep maybe three or four hours, and then I’m up before daylight feeling like I’m ready to get moving.\n\nIt’s strange because I don’t really feel worn out when it happens. I just kind of figure, well, I’m awake, might as well start the day. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you find yourself taking on a lot more projects or activities than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, I suppose. I try to tell myself I’m just being productive, you know, not wasting time. But I’ll start finding extra jobs for myself—redoing route plans that probably didn’t need redoing, checking in on newer drivers after hours, that kind of thing.\n\nAnd at home I’ll notice something that needs fixing and just jump into it. Sitting still too long makes me kind of antsy. I don’t mean it in a bad way… it just feels like I should be doing something. Does that count?\n\nDoctor: When you’re in those fired-up periods, do you find yourself talking more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that one as much. I mean, I probably do talk a little more when I’m in a good mood or trying to get things done, but I wouldn’t say I’m just running my mouth nonstop or anything.\n\nMy wife might have a better read on that than me, honestly. I don’t think it’s been a big thing… at least not that I’ve picked up on. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up times, do you feel more confident or capable than usual?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound like I think I’m better than everybody or anything, but during those times I do get this feeling like… I can handle more than I probably should.\n\nLike, I got it in my head once that I could pretty much rebuild the whole driver training program myself and make it run smoother than the bosses had it. Looking back, that’s not really my lane, and maybe I was getting ahead of myself. But at the time it felt like, “Why not me? I can fix this.” Is that the kind of confidence you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you do things that are risky or out of character for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any big way, anyway. I mean, I might take on too much or act like I’ve got more energy than I really do, but I’m not out there doing anything wild or dangerous, if that’s what you mean.\n\nMaybe I’d have to think about what counts as risky. I don’t want to gloss over something if it matters, but nothing jumps out to me as really out of character like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I can fix this.” Is that the kind of confidence you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you do things that are risky or out of character for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any big way, anyway. I don’t want to gloss over something if it matters, but nothing jumps out to me as really out of character like that.\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you find yourself doing a lot more than usual or feeling like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that part does fit some. So I guess, yes, it does last several days sometimes — if that’s what you’re looking for.\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do they cause problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: I mean… not huge problems, I don’t think. I’m still getting to work and taking care of things. But yeah, there have been some issues.\n\nAt work, I’ve had dispatch or even clients have to repeat details that I really should’ve kept track of the first time. And a couple sales follow-ups kind of slipped through because I was chasing too many things at once, if that makes sense.\n\nAt home… I don’t like saying this, but my wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells around me sometimes. I’m trying to help, not make things harder, but I can tell I’m adding some strain. I don’t know if that counts as “problems,” but… yeah, probably.\n\n[Last Question]\nDid these changes start after your daughter moved back home?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 50 / driver_sales_worker_or_truck_driver / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Duration\nmanifestation: \"This has been going on for about seven months, since my daughter moved back home and I started trying to keep everything steady.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Sitting still too long makes me kind of antsy. I don’t mean it in a bad way… it just feels like I should be doing something. Does that count?\n\nDoctor: When you’re in those fired-up periods, do you find yourself talking more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that one as much. I mean, I probably do talk a little more when I’m in a good mood or trying to get things done, but I wouldn’t say I’m just running my mouth nonstop or anything.\n\nMy wife might have a better read on that than me, honestly. I don’t think it’s been a big thing… at least not that I’ve picked up on. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up times, do you feel more confident or capable than usual?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yeah. Looking back, that’s not really my lane, and maybe I was getting ahead of myself. But at the time it felt like, “Why not me? I can fix this.” Is that the kind of confidence you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you do things that are risky or out of character for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any big way, anyway. I don’t want to gloss over something if it matters, but nothing jumps out to me as really out of character like that.\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you find yourself doing a lot more than usual or feeling like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that part does fit some. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When those fired-up periods happen, do they last for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they can stretch out for a few days at a time. I just kind of figure, well, I’m awake, might as well start the day. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you find yourself taking on a lot more projects or activities than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, I suppose. Sitting still too long makes me kind of antsy. I don’t mean it in a bad way… it just feels like I should be doing something. I mean, I probably do talk a little more when I’m in a good mood or trying to get things done, but I wouldn’t say I’m just running my mouth nonstop or anything.\n\nMy wife might have a better read on that than me, honestly. I don’t think it’s been a big thing… at least not that I’ve picked up on. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up times, do you feel more confident or capable than usual?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yeah. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When those fired-up periods happen, do they last for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they can stretch out for a few days at a time. Maybe more stressed or tired than depressed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you feel unusually powerful, important, or especially capable compared with your usual self?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little… I don’t want to make it sound like I thought I was king of the world or anything.\n\nBut yeah, during those times I did get this idea in my head that I could basically rebuild the whole driver training program myself. Like I could make it run smoother than the bosses ever could, even though… honestly, that’s not really my lane. At the time it felt like, “Why hasn’t anybody fixed this? I can do it.”\n\nI know that probably sounds kind of full of myself. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m taking on anything crazy, but I start finding extra jobs for myself.\n\nLike I’ll be rewriting route plans, checking in on new drivers after hours, or fixing little things around the house that probably could’ve waited. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I don’t think so… not in any big way, anyway.

I don’t think it’s been a big thing… at least not that I’ve picked up on. Looking back, that’s not really my lane, and maybe I was getting ahead of myself. But at the time it felt like, “Why not me? I can fix this.” Is that the kind of confidence you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you do things that are risky or out of character for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any big way, anyway. I mean, I might take on too much or act like I’ve got more energy than I really do, but I’m not out there doing anything wild or dangerous, if that’s what you mean.\n\nMaybe I’d have to think about what counts as risky. I don’t want to gloss over something if it matters, but nothing jumps out to me as really out of character like that.\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you find yourself doing a lot more than usual or feeling like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that part does fit some. I wouldn’t say I’m bouncing off the walls, exactly, but I do start finding extra things to do.\n\nLike at work I’ll start rewriting route plans or checking in on newer drivers after hours, stuff that probably isn’t really required. And at home I’ll notice things that need fixing and suddenly I’m working on those too.\n\nIf I sit down too long, I get kind of antsy, like I should be up doing something. I don’t know if that’s the sort of thing you mean, but yeah, I do get more active than usual.\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do your thoughts race?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it racing, exactly. My mind gets busy, I guess — like I’m thinking about a bunch of things I could do or should take care of — but I don’t know if it’s out of control or anything.\n\nMaybe I’ve just always thought of that as being focused or motivated. If “racing” means I can’t keep up with my own thoughts, I’m not sure I’ve really noticed it like that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When those fired-up periods happen, do they last for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they can stretch out for a few days at a time. It’s not like every single day is dramatic or anything, but it’s been kind of a pattern over the last seven months or so.\n\nIt started around when my daughter moved back home, and I was trying to keep everything steady. So I guess, yes, it does last several days sometimes — if that’s what you’re looking for.\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do they cause problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: I mean… not huge problems, I don’t think. I’m still getting to work and taking care of things. But yeah, there have been some issues.\n\nAt work, I’ve had dispatch or even clients have to repeat details that I really should’ve kept track of the first time. And a couple sales follow-ups kind of slipped through because I was chasing too many things at once, if that makes sense.\n\nAt home… I don’t like saying this, but my wife and daughter seem like they’re walking on eggshells around me sometimes. I’m trying to help, not make things harder, but I can tell I’m adding some strain. I don’t know if that counts as “problems,” but… yeah, probably.\n\nDoctor: Did these changes start after your daughter moved back home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s about right. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like everything changed overnight, but it’s been going on for maybe seven months now — around when my daughter moved back home.\n\nI think I started trying really hard to keep everything steady, and that’s when I noticed these periods more. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times during these past months when you felt down or depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it depressed, exactly. I mean, everybody has off days, and with everything at home I’ve definitely felt worn down sometimes.\n\nBut “down most of the day”... I’m not sure. I haven’t really been tracking it like that. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Maybe more stressed or tired than depressed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you feel unusually powerful, important, or especially capable compared with your usual self?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little… I don’t want to make it sound like I thought I was king of the world or anything.\n\nBut yeah, during those times I did get this idea in my head that I could basically rebuild the whole driver training program myself. Like I could make it run smoother than the bosses ever could, even though… honestly, that’s not really my lane. At the time it felt like, “Why hasn’t anybody fixed this? I can do it.”\n\nI know that probably sounds kind of full of myself. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you find yourself starting a lot more projects or tasks than you normally would?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m taking on anything crazy, but I start finding extra jobs for myself.\n\nLike I’ll be rewriting route plans, checking in on new drivers after hours, or fixing little things around the house that probably could’ve waited. It feels useful at the time, like I’m being productive, but I do get kind of antsy if I sit still too long.\n\nIs that what you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you spend more money than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any big way, anyway.\n\nI mean, I might grab something for the house or pick up lunch without thinking too hard about it, but I haven’t noticed myself going on spending sprees or buying things I couldn’t afford. My wife might have a better eye on that than me, but from my side, no, I wouldn’t say money spending has been a thing.\n\nDoctor: During those fired-up periods, do you get much more done than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do get more done, or at least I’m trying to.\n\nIt’s not like I’m doing anything amazing, but I’ll keep finding extra things that need doing—rewriting route plans, checking on new drivers after hours, fixing stuff around the house. At the time it feels like I’m just being responsible, you know?\n\nBut yeah, I guess compared to normal, I’m a lot more active. I get kind of antsy if I just sit there. Is that the kind of “more done” you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you’re not in one of those fired-up periods, do you still sleep only three or four hours a night?\n\nPatient: Yeah… honestly, most nights I’m probably only getting three or four hours.\n\nI don’t know if that sounds worse than it is, because I don’t always feel bad from it. I’ll wake up before daylight and feel like I’m ready to get going, not dragging or anything. I’ve kind of treated it like that’s just how I am, but… yeah, that’s pretty common for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping only three or four hours a night like this for most of your adult life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so, for a long time anyway.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most nights I’m probably only sleeping three or four hours. I’ll wake up before daylight and feel ready to roll, not like I’m worn out. I’ve kind of always figured that was just how I was built, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
